# Projects, with the city record that documents them

Generated 2026-11-24 by `tools/projects/project-record.py`. **Do not edit this file by hand — the next run overwrites it.**

This gives a project one place to stand, whichever record it came from, and links in what the city has published about it. Two kinds of project, because an advocate meets both:

- **224 SFMTA projects**, live from its ArcGIS project layer. These are the bikeways themselves.
- **383 Public Works coordinated projects**, the repaving and utility jobs that will open a street. Public Works publishes no page for these — the rows come out of an internal system called DotMaps, which has no public address — so the dataset row is the whole published record, and that is what the link below reaches. 186 of them sit on the plan's network with nothing published about bike scope.

Also matched, by name and contract number only: 195 SFMTA project pages not on its map and 99 Public Works project pages in progress, so a row the site lists from one of them carries its record.

Linked in are the two records that legislate a bikeway: [engineering hearing orders](../sfmta-hearings/bike-items.md) and [SFMTA Board items](../sfmta-board/bike-items.md), both read from **2010-01-01**, the day the [Biking and Rolling Plan](https://www.sfmta.com/projects/biking-and-rolling-plan) was adopted.

## What a link means depends on which project it is on

| On | The link says | Why it differs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| an SFMTA project | *authorises this project* | the order or minute is the act that built it |
| a Public Works job | *concerns a street this project will open* | the city has legislated a bikeway on a street this job will dig up. That is worth reading before the paving starts. It is **not** evidence the paving includes the bikeway |

## How far each link can be trusted

A street name on its own is not enough. One board item about the Bayview Community Pathway names Third Street, and 22 projects mention Third Street somewhere on their page. So every link is graded, and only the first three grades are counted anywhere in this file. A match on the street alone is shown as same street and never counted: a hand-checked sample on 2026-09-29 found none of ten such links was about its project.

| Grade | What matched | Links |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **exact** | the record names the project in words | 2459 |
| **strong** | the record names a program and this project's street, and the project is that program's by name | 26 |
| **probable** | a contract award on the project's streets for its kind of work, in its dates | 29 |
| *same street, not counted* | the record is on the project's street and does not name the project | 482 |
| *ambiguous, not counted* | as good as strong, but it matched more than one project of the same kind | 82 |
| *weak, not counted* | the corridor street appears somewhere on the page | 2052 |

Ambiguous is the honest limit. 4th Street Pedestrian Safety and 4th Street Transit Improvement are both projects on 4th Street, so an order on 4th Street matches both and at most one is right. Separating them needs the order's extent geocoded against each project's geometry, which is the next thing to build.

## How much of the record a reader can actually reach

| Question | Answer |
| --- | --- |
| SFMTA projects carrying a record worth trusting | **190 of 224** (85%) |
| Public Works jobs worth asking about that carry one | **37 of 186** (20%) |
| Every project here that carries a document link of any kind | **432 of 946** (46%) |
| On-plan jobs where every planned facility has the plan's own written definition | **175 of 210** (83%) |
| On-plan jobs where only some of them do | **19 of 210** (9%) |

The last two rows are the plan's side rather than the city's. Separated, Shared and Shared L3 carry a quotable definition. These do not, and a job that crosses one of them cannot be scored against the plan at all:

| Undefined facility | On-plan jobs naming it |
| --- | --- |
| Undecided | 25 |
| Shared-use Path | 9 |
| Car-free | 6 |

The plan uses **Undecided** on 32 of Appendix L's 393 rows and never defines it — the word appears only as a map-legend label. **Car-free** is one the plan's own Action 2.7.1 promises to define and has not.

## What the records that match no project are

124 of 639 records attach to no project, and that is mostly correct rather than a miss: a bikeshare dock or a red curb is legislated on its own and belongs to no project.

| The record authorises | Count |
| --- | --- |
| something else | 47 |
| a bikeway class change | 27 |
| a turn or closure rule | 20 |
| a parking or stopping rule | 17 |
| a red or coloured zone | 11 |
| a signal, crossing or hump | 2 |

## What this cannot tell you

- **Whether a completed project was legislated inside this window.** SFMTA publishes no construction or completion date — the only date on a project is when somebody last edited its web page, which moves when a webmaster fixes a typo. So a project finished in 2021 and a project finished last month are indistinguishable here, and any "share of completed projects carrying a record" computed from this data would be measuring page edits. That date is the missing input.
- **Whether a linked record is the whole authorisation.** A project is legislated in several items across several hearings. This finds the ones it can; it does not know when it has them all.
- **Anything before 2010-01-01.** Both readers start there.
